# Safety matrix, run 4 step 4 (verdicts 1 to 98): the six group tables in one file

Merged by the lead from `matrix-W1.md` .. `matrix-W6.md` (kept beside it), unchanged, in this order:

- [Safety matrix, writer W1: rules R1, R2, R3, R13](#w1)
- [Safety matrix, group W2: R4, R5, R6, R17 (verdicts 1 to 97)](#w2)
- [Safety matrix, group W3: R7 (kept views) and R8 (views and changes, checked when compiling)](#w3)
- [Safety matrix, writer W4: R9, R10, R11 (several owners and shared links; a view of an object reached through a link; `weakref` links and parent links)](#w4)
- [Safety matrix, writer W5: R12 (function values)](#w5)
- [Safety matrix, group W6: R14, R15, R16 and the C side](#w6)

<a id="w1"></a>

## Safety matrix, writer W1: rules R1, R2, R3, R13

Brought to verdicts 1 to 97 by agent 05 (test updater T1) of run 4, step 4, from step 3's table (agent
07's, verdicts 1 to 82). Rules from `V5/rules.md` (R1 lines 105-117, R2 119-162, R3 164-245, R13 788-804;
section 0 lines 14-101; section 2 lines 964-1026; section 3 lines 1028-1125; section 4 lines 1127-1215),
words from `V5/vocabulary.md`, the owner's verdicts in `inputs/JOB.md`, the lead's worklist
(`inputs/worklist.md`), the spelling script's report (`V5/tools/step4_spell_report.md`), step 3's checks
(`V5/safety/recheck.md`, `V5/readability.md`) and the answers named in each row. Nothing was compiled or
run. The tests are in `V5/conformance/R1/`, `R2/`, `R3/` and `R13/`: 171 files (R1 29, R2 44, R3 79, R13
19; 80 `accept_`, 82 `reject_`, 9 `run_`, counted with `ls`). Every test's `## ` comment quotes its
guarantee and names its source; every refused line carries `#$ error[CODE]: ...` and the file's
`#$ help:` gives the line to write, which compiles in that program (R18, verdict 73).

How a result is verified once the model is built, for every row:
- `compile-fail`: the conformance runner (`tests/conformance`, today's harness in
  `compiler/ember_driver/tests/`) checks the code and the message on the marked line, and the `#$ help:`
  text; one mistake gives one message (R18), so a second diagnostic fails the test.
- `compile-pass` / `run-pass`: the runner checks that it compiles with no error and that stdout equals
  the `#$ stdout:` lines.
- `run_` tests (the run-time rows of section 2): the same stdout check, and the same program built with
  AddressSanitizer and LeakSanitizer (today's sanitizer profile of `ember_rt`), which must report
  nothing: each block allocated and freed once, no read of freed memory.

"Enforced by" names the part that would enforce the rule and today's source file doing that kind of
work: parser (`compiler/ember_parser/src/body.rs`, `decls.rs`), type checker
(`compiler/ember_typeck/src/lib.rs`, `errors.rs`, `habits.rs`), borrow check (the MIR pass:
`compiler/ember_mir/src/lower.rs`, `compiler/ember_analysis/src/borrows.rs` for views and E3060/E3021,
`drops.rs` for moves and drop flags, `regions.rs` and `stores.rs` for lifetimes and E3063), effect
inference (today's effect sets, `[EFF-4]`, `compiler/ember_analysis/src/effects.rs`, which since verdict
86 works out what every function changes and reads from its body, with no clause to check), code
generator (`compiler/ember_codegen_c/src/lib.rs`, `compiler/ember_codegen_llvm/src/arenas.rs`,
`closures.rs`), runtime (`runtime/ember_rt/src/ember_rt.c`).

What changed in this step, by row (each row says which verdict): R1-1, R1-2, R1-3, R1-7, R1-12, R1-13
(rewritten), R1-14; R2-2, R2-12, R2-17 (rewritten); R3-1, R3-3, R3-5, R3-8, R3-10, R3-12, R3-14
(rewritten), R3-16, R3-17, R3-18, and the new rows R3-19 (verdict 85) and R3-20 (verdict 97); R13-4. The
deletions, renames and additions are listed at the end, with their reasons.

### R2. A name for a part is a read-only view; `=` puts a value in the place a name stands for

| id | guarantee | enforced by | today's tests | missing edge cases | tests | expected result and how verified once built |

| R2-2 | a change through a plain name for a part is refused; `ref mut` before the part makes a name for that very place that may change it | type checker (E3023) | `BRW-1/reject_writing_through_a_shared_ref.em`, `BRW-1/accept_writing_through_a_mut_ref.em` | a part of a part (`pos = p.body.pos`); a method that may change its object (worked out from its body, verdict 86) called through a part name (section 3 line 1038, E3023 "`heal` changes the player") | `reject_a_change_through_a_name_for_a_part`, `accept_ref_mut_names_a_part_that_may_change` | compile-fail E3023 "`pos.x += 1` changes `p.pos` through `pos`, which only reads it" with AN-44's help (`pos = copy of p.pos` compiles on a `@derive(Copy)` `Vec2`, verdict 68); accept stdout `2` |

## Safety matrix, group W2: R4, R5, R6, R17 (verdicts 1 to 97)

Run 4, step 4, agent 05 (test updater T1), from step 3's table (agent 08's, verdicts 1 to 82). Rules R4
(parameters and calls), R5 (a function's first line, and what the compiler works out from its body), R6
(views a call gives) and R17 (inferred facts at a package's edge), with the refusals of section 3 and the
run-time checks of section 2 that belong to them, brought to verdicts 1 to 97 and the corrected records
of 53 and 58. `V5` = `tasks/96/redesign/run4/v5/`; `rules:N` is line N of `V5/rules.md`. The tests are in
`V5/conformance/R4/`, `R5/`, `R6/`, `R17/`: 159 files (67 `accept_`, 85 `reject_`, 7 `run_`; one
`accept_` is `compile-pass` with a warning), counted with `ls`; `R17/` also holds `ember.toml` and the
package `ledger/` its tests import. Nothing was compiled or run.

What this step changed (all in the four folders), by verdict:
- **86** (`changes` and `reads` removed; the compiler works out what every function changes and reads
  from its code): R5 is rewritten. Of the 20 tests of the report's section 1 that are this group's (R4: 1,
  R5: 19), 12 are deleted because the program now compiles and an existing `accept_` is that very
  program, and 8 are turned into the inferred check with its chain message (E3020), the E0100 refusal of
  a clause Ember no longer has, E0118 over the parts that remain, or a use after a hand-over (E3040, the
  R4 one) (tables at the end); two more R5 tests whose refusal rested on a removed clause are deleted. Every message, help and comment that named a clause is rewritten; `ledger`'s interface file now
  records what its functions read, so R17's "a `pub` method with no `reads`" test became one refused by
  the recorded fact.
- **84** (`owns` hands over every type): E2085's test is turned into a use after the hand-over (E3040),
  with an `accept_` of `take(copy of n)` and of `take(k)`.
- **87, 88, 89**: the `rename(ref mut LOG[0])` test cites 87 with the rules' message; two `run_` tests and
  one `reject_` for verdict 88; one `accept_` for verdict 89.
- **53's corrected record**: R4-14 no longer counts a call it "cannot see" as using every parameter; the
  test of a stored function refused at the line that stores it is R11's (group T2).
- Worklist section 4: F-13 (E0113 names the caller's `a`), wrong 3-6 (`assert_disjoint`'s import in
  three R4 tests), refusals without a test (`owns this` on `init`, E2108; `fn drop(mut self)`, E1010),
  T-W2 5 (`static fn` and E0113 messages, already right), F-12 (the `@cached` helps that repeated the
  program's line now give the line to remove).

Today's compiler, named in "enforced by" (paths under `compiler/`):
- parser: `ember_parser/src/decls.rs` (first lines, parameter modes; today's E0110), `body.rs` (calls);
- type checker: `ember_typeck/src/lib.rs` (today's E3023, E3027, E2100, E2103, E2140, E3013, E1010);
- borrow check (the MIR pass): `ember_analysis/src/borrows.rs` (today's E3020, E3021, E3022, E3060,
  E3062); moves: `ember_analysis/src/drops.rs` (E3040); fields set in `init`:
  `ember_analysis/src/definite_init.rs` (E2100);
- effect inference: `ember_analysis/src/effects.rs` (today's effect sets, `[EFF-1]`..`[EFF-8]`), which
  works out what each function changes, reads and ends from its body, through its callees, every
  function the program can put in a function value and every override and implementation (verdict 86),
  and the "uses a view parameter after a line that may end or move what it views" fact of verdicts 53, 54
  and 87;
- interface file: `ember_build/src/interface.rs` (today records `effects` per body, `[EFF-4]`; every fact
  above is recorded beside them, R17);
- code generator: `ember_mir/src/lower.rs` (`lower_assert_disjoint`, line 1733; the index compare would
  be inserted here; the copy of a function for one whole variable, verdict 88), `ember_codegen_c/src/lib.rs`
  (today's `restrict` kernels, `restrict_views`);
- runtime: `ember_rt`'s panic (the stops of the index compare and of `assert_disjoint`, with rules
  section 2's texts).

How a result is verified once built: `compile-fail` by the runner's expected `#$ error[CODE]: message`
on the refused line and the `#$ help:` line; `compile-pass` by exit 0 and the `#$ warning[CODE]:` line;
`run-pass` by exit 0 and one `#$ stdout:` line per printed line; `run-fail` by the `#$ panics:` text,
which is rules section 2's stop text in the program's names. The rows marked "C check" also need the
generated C inspected (today's `#$ assert-c:` header), which no test writes yet because the model's C
spelling is not fixed.

### R5. A function's first line, and what the compiler works out from its body

| id | guarantee | enforced by | today's tests | missing edge cases | tests | expected result and how it is verified once built |

### R6. Views a call gives

| id | guarantee | enforced by | today's tests | missing edge cases | tests | expected result and how it is verified once built |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R6-1 | With no `from`, a method's result points into all of its object and a function's into every view argument it could come from: a change of the object while the view is used is refused (E3021), a reshape ends the view (E3060), and a view of a local's result may not be returned (E3063) (rules:412-418; AN-22). | borrow check (`ember_analysis/src/borrows.rs`, today's region rules `[LT-1]`) | `LT-1/accept_rule_3_ties_the_return_to_every_view_parameter.em`, `LT-1/reject_mutating_an_argument_while_the_result_lives.em`, `LT-1/reject_using_another_parameter_while_the_result_lives.em`, `BRW-10/reject_a_view_from_a_method_keeps_its_field_borrowed.em` | "into the one, if only one could be the source" (a function with one view argument and a number); a result that may come from a field of an argument's link | `reject_a_method_result_points_into_its_whole_object.em`, `reject_a_word_of_a_local_parser_returned_without_from.em`, `reject_a_function_result_ends_when_either_view_argument_grows.em`, `accept_from_src_lets_the_parser_move_on.em` | compile-fail E3021 (its first way out `fn next_word() -> StrView from src:`, without step 3's `changes pos`, verdict 86), E3063 (AN-22's message, the same line), E3060; run-pass stdout `let x let` |
| R6-2 | `-> T from name` narrows the result to one parameter, or one field of `this` written bare; the body may give back only a view of what `from` names (E3062); a name that is both a parameter and a field is refused (E2118); the old `@borrows` is gone (E0104) (rules:414-416, 1076-1077, 1113, 1144). | borrow check (E3062, today's), type checker (E2118, new), parser (E0104) | `LT-1a/accept_borrows_narrows_the_region.em`, `LT-1a/reject_borrows_names_a_field_but_returns_the_receiver.em`, `LT-1a/reject_named_borrows_argument.em` (`@borrows`, now `from`) | `from` naming a parameter that is not a view | `reject_a_result_outside_what_its_from_names.em`, `reject_from_names_a_parameter_and_a_field.em`, `reject_borrows_is_now_from.em`, `accept_from_narrows_a_function_result_to_one_parameter.em`, `accept_from_src_lets_the_parser_move_on.em` | compile-fail E3062 (rules:1076; help `fn next_word(line: StrView) -> StrView from line:`), E2118 (rules:1077's fifth, both lines without `changes pos`), E0104 (completeness G8's, naming the line it removes); run-pass stdout `2` |
| R6-3 | Pointing into an argument means into it and everything it owns: a reshape of a list it owns ends the view (E3060), and any other change of it while the view is used is refused (E3021), as Rust's borrow of the whole argument (rules:416-419). | borrow check | `LT-18/accept_independent_field_borrow_ends_before_mutation.em`, `LT-19/reject_mutation_while_either_same_source_field_is_live.em` (today keeps fields apart: R6 is wider) | the argument reached through a link (the view does not go through it, rules:37-39) | `reject_a_change_to_any_part_of_the_argument_while_its_view_is_used.em`, `reject_a_reshape_of_a_list_the_argument_owns_ends_the_view.em`, `accept_the_argument_changes_after_the_views_last_use.em` | compile-fail E3021, E3060 (its way out the line to move); run-pass stdout `key` / `2 1` |

## Safety matrix, group W3: R7 (kept views) and R8 (views and changes, checked when compiling)

Run 4, step 4, agent 06 (test updater for group T2), brought to verdicts 1 to 97 and the corrected
records of verdicts 53 and 58. `V5` = `tasks/96/redesign/run4/v5/`; "line N" without a file is a line of
`V5/rules.md`; "verdict N" is the owner's verdict N in `V5/inputs/JOB.md`; "AN-N" is the answer in
`V5/area-*.md`; "the worklist" is `V5/inputs/worklist.md`; "G-N" is gap N of `V5/safety/completeness.md`.
Nothing was compiled or run (brief, "Machine limits"). Tests are in `V5/conformance/R7/` (46 files) and
`V5/conformance/R8/` (110 files): 73 `accept_`, 80 `reject_`, 3 `run_`. Every `reject_` file marks the
refused line with `#$ error[CODE]: message` and gives a line of that message in `#$ help:`; every "line N"
a message names was checked to point at a code line of its file, and every `#$ help:` text was found in
its message (`target/redesign7/06/`, the check after `r8_edits.py`). The files as this step found them are
in `target/redesign7/06/orig/`; the edits were made by `target/redesign7/06/r7_edits.py` and
`r8_edits.py` (helpers in `lib.py`: every replacement must match exactly once, and a comment block whose
length changes shifts the `line N` references below it).

**How every row is verified once the model is built.** `compile-fail`: the conformance runner checks
the code and the message on the marked line (and the `#$ help:` text). `run-pass`: the runner compares
stdout with `#$ stdout:`. `run-fail`: the runner checks the panic starts with `#$ panics:`.
`#$ assert-c-count:` lines are checked on the emitted C by today's runner
(`compiler/ember_driver/tests/milestones.rs`, `assert_c_count`). Where a row guards memory (a moved
buffer, a freed text, a freed block), its accepted programs are also run once under AddressSanitizer
(the C backend with Clang `-fsanitize=address`), which must report nothing: that shows the accepted
program is not only accepted but safe.

**What a change is, since verdict 86.** No first line says what a function changes or reads, and no
function type says it either. Every row below that uses "what a call may change, end or reshape" gets that
fact from the code, as R5 (lines 362-384) says: the callee's own lines, the functions it calls, every
function the program can put in a function value it calls (R12, lines 662-671), and the `drop` of every
object it may end; the message names the chain of calls (R18, lines 952-954).

### The matrix

| id | guarantee | enforced by | today's tests | missing edge cases | tests | expected result and how it is verified once built |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R7-1 | A struct that holds a view (`ref T`, `ref mut T`, `sharedref mut T`, `Span[T]`, `MutSpan[T]`, `StrView`, or a struct that holds one) is a view, and lives no longer than what its view fields point into, whether or not it says `@view` (lines 427-429; `[TYP-34]`). | type checker marks the type a view (today's inference in `compiler/ember_typeck/src/lib.rs`, `[TYP-34]`); borrow check bounds its life (`compiler/ember_analysis/src/regions.rs`, E3063 in `compiler/ember_analysis/src/stores.rs:462`, `:522`) | `TYP-34/accept_a_view_struct_needs_no_attribute.em`, `TYP-34/accept_view_through_a_type_declared_later.em`, `TYP-15/accept_a_view_in_a_local.em`, `BCK-4/reject_a_loan_outliving_its_place.em`, `LT-1/reject_a_local_views_target_escaping.em` | a struct holding a struct that holds a view; a struct with a `MutSpan` field; a view struct sent to a thread (E7015, R15, W6's) | `R7/accept_a_struct_holding_a_view_needs_no_view_mark.em`, `R7/reject_a_struct_holding_a_view_returned_past_its_source.em`, `R7/accept_a_struct_holding_a_view_of_an_argument_may_be_returned.em` | run-pass `3 2`; compile-fail E3063 on the `return` line ("the `Holder` returned here holds a view of `xs` ..."); run-pass `3` |

### What step 4 changed in this table and its tests

- **Verdict 86 (no `changes`, no `reads`).** The script (`V5/tools/step4_spell.py`) took the clauses off 75
  lines (`fn` lines, function types, lambdas) in 46 files of these two directories; every one was checked
  against the report's section 3 and is right as code, but in 2 of them the same line's `#$` message still
  quoted the removed words (`R7/reject_from_naming_both_a_parameter_and_a_field.em` line 11,
  `R8/reject_a_library_call_given_a_function_that_changes_the_walked_list.em` line 17), corrected here.
  What it could not do is done here, in 43 existing files and the two renamed ones: the comments that
  explained a written `changes T` (verdicts 63, 64) or "its first line says" now say that the compiler
  works the change out (R5), or drop the sentence; the messages of 9 files that quoted a clause or a function type's
  `changes` now name the chain of calls (R18): `hook()` "(it may run `note`, given on line 20 ...)",
  `on_hit(ref mut enemies)` "(it may run `drop_last`, given on line 23 ...)", `p.heal()` "(through
  `heal`, `this.hp += 1`, line 14)", `this.add_player(...)` "(through `add_player`, line 16)", `b.add(4)`
  ("`add` pushes to `this.items` (line 11)"), the library call's (section 3's line 1066 message), the
  `drop`'s ("its line 14"), the two `next_word` first lines of the E2118 message, and the E3022 message
  of `R7/reject_two_kept_ref_mut_fields_of_one_player.em` (section 3's own form, without
  `fn main() changes Player:`).
- **Tests whose refusal belonged to a removed word.** `R8/reject_a_function_that_ends_an_enemy_without_naming_log.em`
  (E2105, the report's section 1) is deleted: nothing is left of a written word to refuse. Its program's
  real danger, a call of `reset` inside a loop over `LOG`, is the new
  `R8/reject_a_function_that_ends_an_enemy_called_inside_a_walk_of_log.em` (E3020 with the chain), and
  `accept_a_function_that_ends_an_enemy_names_log.em` became
  `accept_a_function_that_ends_an_enemy_called_after_a_walk_of_log.em`. One more refusal belonged to a
  removed word without the report listing it: `R7/reject_a_method_that_may_change_the_target_inside_a_loop_over_it.em`
  refused `cam.shake()` because `changes target` covered the whole player; worked out from its body,
  `shake` writes only `hp`, which a walk of the inventory allows, so that program is now the accepted
  `R7/accept_a_method_that_changes_a_number_of_the_target_inside_a_loop_over_its_list.em`, and the
  reject's method clears the inventory (`empty_target`).

## Safety matrix, writer W4: R9, R10, R11 (several owners and shared links; a view of an object reached through a link; `weakref` links and parent links)

Brought to verdicts 1 to 97 and the corrected records of verdicts 53 and 58 in run 4, step 4 (agent 06,
test updater for group T2). Sources: `V5/rules.md` section 0 (lines 14-101: link 40-42, reshape 46-51,
ended 72-73, count pair 74-75, deferred-drop region 76-78, unseen code 86-89), R5 (lines 353-410: what the
compiler works out from a body, 362-384), R9 (lines 536-557), R10 (lines 559-584), R11 (lines 586-659),
R12 (lines 661-786: its own state, 681-691), section 2 (lines 964-1026: its rows 971-996, its stop texts
1001-1011, the count-limit paragraph 1013-1018), section 3 (lines 1028-1125), section 4 (lines 1127-1215);
`V5/vocabulary.md` rows `shares`, `sharedref`, `weakref`, `Weak`, `RefCell`, `exists as`, `drop`,
`upgrade`, `mem.forget`; verdicts 1 to 97 of `V5/inputs/JOB.md` (here chiefly 53, 55, 56, 59, 77, 79, 82,
86, 96); `V5/inputs/worklist.md` (sections 1 and 4); `V5/readability.md` (F-01, F-06, F-16) and
`V5/safety/recheck.md`. `C` = `tasks/96/redesign/run4/v5/conformance/`. Today's tests are under
`tests/conformance/`. Nothing was compiled or run. The files as this step found them are in
`target/redesign7/06/orig/`; the edits were made by `target/redesign7/06/r9_edits.py` and
`r10_r11_edits.py` (helpers in `lib.py`).

Today's source files named below: the type checker `compiler/ember_typeck/src/lib.rs` (today's E3023,
E2020, E3040, E7004); the borrow check `compiler/ember_analysis/src/borrows.rs` (today's E3020, E3021,
E3060, E3063, with `regions.rs`); the count elision `compiler/ember_analysis/src/uncounted_handles.rs`
(`[RC-3]`, `[RC-2c]`); the cycle lint `compiler/ember_analysis/src/cycles.rs` (L3001); drops and
hand-overs `compiler/ember_analysis/src/drops.rs` (E3013, E3040); the code generator
`compiler/ember_codegen_c/src/lib.rs`; the runtime `runtime/ember_rt/include/ember_rt.h`
(`ember_retain_plain` / `ember_release_plain` lines 1528-1539, `ember_weak_retain` / `ember_weak_release`
/ `ember_weak_upgrade` lines 1486-1488, the header's `weak` field line 1428). E7007, E7014, E7015, E7017,
E2116, E2119 and E3031 are not in today's compiler (rules section 4's bold list).

**What a change is, since verdict 86.** No first line and no function type says what a function changes;
the compiler works it out from the code (R5 lines 362-384), a call through a function value by every
function the program can put there (R12 lines 662-671), and the message names the chain (R18). So no
`main` or function in `C/R9`, `C/R10`, `C/R11` writes `changes T` for a change made through a link (what
verdicts 63 and 64 asked for is void), and a refusal that rested on a function type's `changes Player` or
`changes Node` now rests on the function the program stores there. A `drop` writes `fn drop():`
(verdicts 69, 86). Messages give every line they replace, and their fixes compile (R18, verdict 73).

Two of these checks stop the program, each with a `run_fail_` test and its stop text from section 2's
table, in the 8-bit form of the paragraph under it (lines 1013-1018): "too many owners of a `Player` (255)"
and "too many `weakref` links to a `Player` (255)". The others are verified by their stdout, by reading the
emitted C for the operation the row names, and by an AddressSanitizer (ThreadSanitizer for the thread
tests) run with no report. Completeness gap G15 (a `weakref` read inside R10's region) has no program
left: R10 places no region; inside R11's walk region the same read is the walk's own next step, which
finds the set-aside node ended (`C/R11/run_a_walk_whose_visit_may_end_nodes_stops_at_the_first_ended_parent.em`).

### Refusals of section 3 covered here

E3023 (a change through a `shares` owner or a `shares` field; a plain parameter behind a `shares` link),
E3020 (a shared list or a `sharedref mut` target's list changed during a loop through another owner or
holder), E3021 (a shared element under a read-only view; the walk with views), E3060 (a removal through
another owner; a name opened from a link used after a call or a loop of calls that may end its object, by
its arguments or by the function stored in a function value; a view of what a replaced function value's
state owned), E3031 (a stored visit function that ends its node and then reads it, at the storing line),
E7014, E7015 (a `shares` owner, a `sharedref mut` holder), E7007 (two tasks through two `sharedref mut`
links with `=`), E1010 (`RefCell`, `upgrade`), E2020 (a link used unopened; an object stored without
`weakref`), E2116 (`copy of` a link), E2119 (a `weakref` of an element stored inline, and of a struct
field), E3013 (a strong parent field), E2200 (a struct viewing its parent), and the warning L3001. Each
has a `reject_` test and an `accept_` (or `run_`) test of the nearest allowed program. Left to other
writers: E0114 and E3063 for `sharedref mut` (R7, W3); E3023 for `shares mut x` made from a read-only
owner (R3, W1); E7004, E7017 and E7018 (R15, W6); a function value's own state ended inside a walk of
what it changes (R12, W5).

### What step 4 changed in this table and its tests

- **Verdict 86 (no `changes`, no `reads`).** The script (`V5/tools/step4_spell.py`) took the clauses off 84
  lines in 47 files of these three directories (R9: 45 lines in 31 files; R10: 8 in 4; R11: 31 in 12),
  and `with` off nine captures (verdict 83: six in R9, three in R11); every line was checked against the report's section 3 and
  is right as code, but in 2 of them the same line's `#$` message still quoted the removed words
  (`C/R9/reject_an_unscoped_thread_holding_a_sharedref_link.em` line 17, `scope.spawn(fn() changes Player:
  ...)` in its way out; `C/R9/reject_two_tasks_set_one_player_through_two_sharedref_links.em` line 21, the
  Atomic way out with `fn main() changes Atomic[int]:`), corrected here (the second now gives the lock,
  below). Two refusals rested on a function type's clause that the script removed, so that each program

  Player`" or gave a first line with `changes Player` are corrected (R9-3, R10-4).

<a id="w5"></a>

## Safety matrix, writer W5: R12 (function values)

Run 4, step 4 (agent 07, test updater for group T3), brought to verdicts 1 to 97. Rule: R12,
`V5/rules.md` lines 661-786, with the section 3 refusals and the section 2 run-time rows that belong to
it. Verdicts from `V5/inputs/JOB.md`; settled points from `V5/inputs/worklist.md` and `V5/changes.md`.
Tests: `V5/conformance/R12/`, 100 tests (43 `reject_`, 52 `accept_`, 5 `run_`) and one module the tests
import (`widgets.em`). Nothing was compiled or run (brief, "Machine limits"). `V5` =
`tasks/96/redesign/run4/v5/`; "line N" with no file is a line of `V5/rules.md`. Step 3's matrix and the
R12 folder as this step found them are kept at `target/redesign7/07/orig/`.

What this step changed, in short: verdict 86 removed `changes`, `reads`, `captured` and change lists, so
the 31 R12 tests of the spelling report's section 1 (their expected error, E2105 or E2113, was a missing
or wrong clause) were each made the nearest test of what the program now does (12) or deleted where the
program had become an existing `accept_` test (19); every other test lost its clauses (the script's
work) and got comments and messages in the v5 words, line numbers and chain messages (R18, F-12); four
tests whose refusal came from a function type now read the stored functions instead (the handler that
causes the refusal is now in the program); verdict 83 (no `with` before captures) and verdict 84 (`owns`
on a number) have their tests; step 3's hole 3 (parts 1 and 2) and hole 6a's original form have a
`reject_` and an `accept_` each; the rows are renumbered to follow v5's R12. Full list at the end.

### How to read this

- One section per guarantee, `R12-1` .. `R12-26`, with the seven fields of the brief.
- **today's tests** are `tests/conformance/<RULE>/<file>.em`. Those that expect the opposite of the new
  rule are marked **(superseded)**.
- **enforced by** names today's source file that does this kind of work now. Short names used below:
  - `typeck`: `compiler/ember_typeck/src/lib.rs` (the type checker; captures are resolved at
    `resolve_capture`, about line 25452, and checked from about line 49099).
  - `effects`: `compiler/ember_analysis/src/effects.rs` (today's effect sets, `[EFF-1]`..`[EFF-6]`,
    worked out from bodies, as v5 does; it says itself that a call through a function value
    "contributes every effect: no function type declares its effects yet". v5 instead counts every
    function the program stores into that place, the whole program and its libraries: new work here).
  - `borrows`: `compiler/ember_analysis/src/borrows.rs` (the borrow check, the MIR pass that today
    checks borrows and regions; it emits E3020, E3021, E3022, E3060; captures from about line 2257).
  - `stores`: `compiler/ember_analysis/src/stores.rs` (emits E3026 today).
  - `drops`: `compiler/ember_analysis/src/drops.rs` (emits E3013 today).
  - `parser`: `compiler/ember_parser/src/body.rs` and `decls.rs`.
  - `codegen`: `compiler/ember_codegen_c/src/lib.rs` and `compiler/ember_codegen_llvm/src/closures.rs`
    (today's function value is `ember_fn_value`, `{native, descriptor, w[3]}`, 40 bytes).
  - `runtime`: `runtime/ember_rt/src/ember_rt.c`.
  - `interface`: `compiler/ember_build/src/interface.rs` (a package's interface file, R17).
  E0120, E7015 and E2020's lambda-parameter case are not in today's compiler (step 3's search; E7015 is
  a code this model made, rules section 4's note).
- **How a test is verified once built**: a `compile-fail` test by the runner's expected code and
  message on the marked line and its `#$ help:` line (`compiler/ember_driver/tests/milestones.rs`:
  `assert_exact_diagnostics`, a diagnostic no annotation claims fails the test; `assert_diagnostic_helps`,
  each `#$ help:` is text of the diagnostic's help); `run-pass` by `#$ stdout:`; the five `run_` tests,
  and the `accept_` tests that dispatch handlers, also by a build of the emitted C under
  AddressSanitizer with no report, as `V3/area-D.md` AN-60, AN-61 and AN-62 checked the same shapes in C.
- Every message was checked against R18 and verdict 73: the lines it gives compile in that program (the
  `accept_` test named beside each `reject_` test is, in most rows, that program after the fix); every
  line number a message names was checked against its file by a script
  (`target/redesign7/07/check_lines.py`); every `#$ help:` is text of its message. A message about a
  change a call makes names the chain of calls (R18 lines 952-954); none asks for a word on a first line.

---

#### R12-1. A lambda writes nothing about what it changes; the change is worked out and checked where it runs

| field | value |
|---|---|
| guarantee | What a lambda changes (a module variable, a real variable it uses) is worked out from its body, with no word on the lambda or on the functions that run it; a call that runs it inside a loop over what it changes is refused, the message naming the chain. A `changes` clause on a lambda, in the `:` form or the `=>` form, is not Ember (E0100) and the message gives the line without it. |
| source | R12 lines 662-672; R5 lines 362-384; R8 (c) lines 477-494; R18 lines 945-962; verdicts 17, 45, 86; section 3 lines 1051 (E0100, third message's shape), 1065 (E3020) |
| enforced by | `effects` for the change, extended to every function the program stores in the place called; `borrows` for E3020; `parser` for E0100 (today's "unexpected token") |
| today's tests | `EFF-1/accept_a_call_through_a_plain_fn_value_has_the_effects_of_its_functions.em` (effects of a call through a function value worked out, not written: the same approach, by shape today) |
| missing edge cases | a lambda that changes `LOG` only through a call it makes (`fn(): tick()`), covered by the chain but not tested here; a lambda inside a lambda; a named local function (verdict 51) that pushes to `LOG`, run inside a loop over `LOG` |
| tests | `reject_a_lambda_that_pushes_to_log_called_inside_a_loop_over_log.em`, `accept_a_lambda_that_pushes_to_log_called_after_the_loop.em`, `reject_row_6_handler_written_with_changes_score.em`, `reject_an_arrow_lambda_written_with_changes_removed.em`, `accept_an_arrow_lambda_changes_a_variable_it_uses.em`, `accept_a_lambda_changes_an_atomic_it_uses.em`, `accept_a_lambda_that_only_reads_a_variable_it_uses.em` |
| expected result | compile-fail E3020 "`click()` (line 16) pushes to `LOG` (it runs the function made on line 13) while this loop walks `LOG` (line 14); call it after the loop: move line 16, `click()`, below the loop"; run-pass `start`, `2`; compile-fail E0100 "`changes score` is not Ember: the compiler works out what this function changes from its body; write `b.on_click(fn(): score += 1)`" (row 6's own line, nearest allowed `accept_row_6_handler_changes_the_real_score.em`); compile-fail E0100 for `changes removed` before `=>`; run-pass `2 1`; run-pass `2`; run-pass `0` |

## Safety matrix, group W6: R14, R15, R16 and the C side

Run 4, step 4, agent 08 (test updater for group T4), from step 3's table (`V4/safety/matrix-W6.md`, agent
12) brought to verdicts 1 to 97. Rules: R14 (interface values, kind tests, casts), R15 (threads), R16
(`unsafe`, and giving an object to C), and the C side of R12 and R15 (kept callbacks,
`@ffi(no_callbacks)`, the checked trampoline, variadic calls; verdicts 31, 39, 62, 91). Sources:
`V5/rules.md` (cited as `rules.md:N`, the v5 file as agent 01 left it, 08:38 on 2026-10-27),
`V5/vocabulary.md`, `V5/changes.md`, `V5/inputs/JOB.md` (verdicts 1 to 97), `V5/inputs/worklist.md`,
the lead's spelling report `V5/tools/step4_spell_report.md`, `V5/safety/recheck.md` and
`V5/readability.md` (step 3's checks). Nothing was compiled or run.

The tests are in `V5/conformance/R14/` (39), `V5/conformance/R15/` (108, and the C library of the
trampoline and callback tests, `kept_events.c` with `kept_events.h`) and `V5/conformance/R16/` (20, and
the C function of the variadic tests, `wide_sum.c` with `wide_sum.h`): 167 tests (67 `accept_`, 80
`reject_`, 20 `run_`; step 3 had 170: 60, 91, 19). Every file is written by
`target/redesign7/08/build.py` from the label sources in `target/redesign7/08/src/` (step 3's
`tests_r14.py`, `tests_r15.py`, `tests_r15_c.py`, `tests_r16.py`, edited by `target/redesign7/08/edits/`):
a line marked ` @@name` is numbered and `{{name}}` in a message becomes that number, and the lead's own
respelling (`V5/tools/step4_spell.py`) is applied to the code lines, so the 76 files this step leaves
alone come out byte for byte as the lead's script left them (checked: `python -I build.py check` before
any edit printed "174 files: 174 as v5"). Every `line N` a `#$` line names is printed with that line's
code to `target/redesign7/08/lines.txt` and was read against its message; the build also stops on a
refused test with no `#$ error` line or with two.

How every result is verified once the model is built: the conformance runner (`ember test`,
`compiler/ember_driver/src/test_runner.rs`) compiles each file and compares the output with its `#$`
lines: for `compile-fail` the code and the message of the `#$ error` on its line, and `#$ help:`; for
`compile-pass` the `#$ warning` lines; for `run-pass` the stdout lines in order; for `run-fail` the
stdout before the stop and the start of the stop's text (`#$ panics:`). The thread tests are also run
once under ThreadSanitizer and the trampoline, callback, cast and `into_raw` tests under
AddressSanitizer (C backend, Clang), which must report nothing: that shows the run-time checks stop the
program before memory is touched wrongly, and that the compile-time checks leave no data race.

"Enforced by": parser `compiler/ember_parser/src/body.rs`, `decls.rs` (lexer
`compiler/ember_lexer/src/token.rs`); type checker `compiler/ember_typeck/src/lib.rs` (old spellings in
`compiler/ember_typeck/src/habits.rs`); borrow check = the MIR passes of `compiler/ember_analysis/src/`
(`borrows.rs`, `regions.rs`, `stores.rs` for E3026, `coroutines.rs` for E2221, `static_safe.rs` for
E4030, `effects.rs` for today's effect sets, the nearest existing work to the fact verdict 86 asks the
compiler to work out: what every function changes, reads and ends, across function values, `dyn` and
virtual methods and kept callbacks); MIR lowering `compiler/ember_mir/src/lower.rs`
(`lower_class_downcast`), `compiler/ember_mir/src/parallel.rs`; code generator
`compiler/ember_codegen_c/src/lib.rs` (the downcast stop, `AssertKind::Downcast`; the C trampolines),
`compiler/ember_codegen_llvm/src/ffi.rs` and `dispatch.rs`; interface files
`compiler/ember_build/src/interface.rs` (R17); runtime `runtime/ember_rt/src/ember_rt.c`
(`ember_downcast`, locks, thread checks). The codes section 4 marks bold (not in today's
`compiler/ember_diag/src/codes.rs`, `rules.md:1129-1136`) are emitted nowhere today; the row names the
part that would.

### R14: interface values, kind tests and casts

### R15: threads, and the C side

| id | guarantee | enforced by | today's tests | missing edge cases | tests | expected result and how it is verified once built |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R15-1 | Starting a thread counts as making what its function changes, worked out from the program; no first line says it (`rules.md:835-839`; R12 `rules.md:726-739`; R5 `rules.md:362-384`; verdicts 30, 86). The thread rules keep threads apart (R15-2 to R15-13). | the whole-program fact over calls (today's nearest `effects.rs`), from `thread.spawn`'s first line in `std/src/thread.em` (`[R]` above `pub fn spawn(owns f: thread_shared fn() -> R) -> JoinHandle[R]:`, verdict 94) | `THR-10/accept_threads_spawn_join_and_detach.em`; `EFF-2/accept_a_callable_parameter_has_the_passed_functions_effects.em` (effects carried by a function given to a call) | a thread started from a stored function value; a thread that starts a thread; `JobScope` tasks | `R15/accept_main_starts_threads_with_no_word_on_its_first_line.em`, `R15/accept_threads_share_a_log_and_push_under_its_lock.em` | run-pass `1 1 2` and `2` (ThreadSanitizer clean). The E2105 tests of a missing word are gone with verdict 86 (deletions below) |
| R15-2 | A `spawn` inside a loop over what the thread changes under the lock the loop holds is allowed, the thread waiting for the lock; joining it inside that lock, a scope's end included, is refused; the loop's own variable is given `copy of` (verdicts 52, 65, 83; `rules.md:835-839`, `rules.md:861-871`, R12 `rules.md:730-734`, E7018 `rules.md:1099`). | a held-locks analysis over the MIR (new; would sit with `borrows.rs`), with the thread's worked-out changes | none (`THR-4/accept_a_lock_order_inversion_is_reported.em` is the nearest lock analysis) | a join of a handle passed into a function called inside the lock; an `RwLock` read lock held while the thread needs the write lock; a `Mutex[T]` guard held | `R15/accept_a_thread_started_in_a_walk_under_the_lock_it_needs.em`, `R15/reject_a_thread_started_in_a_walk_uses_the_loop_name_without_a_copy.em`, `R15/accept_a_thread_started_under_the_lock_is_joined_after_it.em`, `R15/reject_a_join_inside_the_lock_its_thread_needs.em`, `R15/reject_a_scope_that_ends_inside_the_lock_its_task_needs.em`, `R15/accept_a_scope_started_after_the_lock_block.em` | run-pass `2`, `1`, and `0`, `1` (and the runner's time limit: no hang); compile-fail E7004 for verdict 52's line without a word (section 3's E7004 loop message, `rules.md:1102`, "write `handles.push(thread.spawn(fn() copy of r: worker(r)))` in place of line 24", line 24); E7018 with section 3's message naming the chain ("through `worker`, line 16") and "move line 21 below the block" (line 21); and, for the scope, the message written to R18 that names the two lines it moves (line 22) |
| R15-3 | Scoped tasks need no capture words but for a loop's own variable and a local of one round: `copy of t` gives a task its own copy, and for the loop's name `ref t` lets a scoped task look at this round's element with no copy (verdicts 65, 93); with no word, E3026, whose message gives both words for the loop's name; they use the real variables (`rules.md:846`, `rules.md:861-871`; R3 `rules.md:230-233`; E3026 `rules.md:1095`). | type checker (captures, `ember_typeck/src/lib.rs`); `stores.rs` (E3026) | `THR-11/accept_scoped_tasks_borrow_the_block.em`, `THR-11/accept_join_early_and_function_tasks.em` | a task that only reads a round local (needs its word too); `ref n` of a round local (refused: the round ends first, so E3026's message for a round local gives only `copy of n`); a task given the loop variable through a named local function (verdict 51) | `R15/run_scoped_tasks_add_their_parts_to_a_total.em`, `R15/reject_a_task_uses_the_loop_name_without_a_copy.em`, `R15/accept_a_scoped_task_looks_at_the_loops_text_with_ref.em`, `R15/reject_a_round_local_used_by_a_task_without_a_copy.em`, `R15/accept_a_task_takes_a_copy_of_a_round_local.em` | run-pass `3`, `3`, `3`; compile-fail E3026 with section 3's two-word message, "... in place of line 15" (line 15), and E3026 for the round local, "write `scope.spawn(fn() copy of n: total.fetch_add(n))` in place of line 16" (line 16) |
| R15-4 | Every task's effects, worked out from its body, are checked against the other tasks and the parent until the join: two tasks changing one place other than by a reduction, under a lock or through an `Atomic`, a task that reads such a place first, a read of a reduction's place before the join, and an update that is no reduction are E7007; a task that reads first needs a lock (E7007's message gives only the lock, F-10); reductions on a local or on a field of an object the tasks reach, through two `sharedref mut` links included, are allowed (verdict 77), whole-number and decimal (verdict 92) (`rules.md:846-860`, E7007 `rules.md:1104`, `rules.md:1197`; A note for the rule writer and the checkers (no test depends on it beyond what the tests say): section 3's
C-call E3020 message (`rules.md:1066`) says the kept callback "changes `game.names` (through
`add_point`, line 6)", but in AN-78's program (`V5/area-E.md` AN-78 NEW) `add_point` changes only
`score`. Under verdict 86 the fact is worked out from the body, field by field (R5 `rules.md:385-388`,
row 3), so a callback that changes only `game.score` does not change the walked `game.names`, and the C
call inside that walk is allowed (`R15/accept_a_c_call_inside_a_walk_of_names_when_the_kept_callback_changes_only_the_score.em`).
The refused tests therefore give `add_point` a second line, `this.names.push(String("point"))`.

### What this step changed (run 4, step 4)

The base is step 3's 170 tests (`V4/conformance/R14/`, `R15/`, `R16/`, copied into `V5`, then respelled
by the lead's script: 279 lines in 105 of these files, every one checked against the report's section 3
and found right; none needed correcting). Every further change, with its cause.

#### Changed in place

Checked and left as they were: no test of these rules hands a number over with `owns` (verdict 84), uses
`mem.forget`, `ForeignBox`, a map or list changed through its own name inside its loop (verdicts 58, 91,
95), a count limit (verdict 96, W4's) or a value made later for an `owns` field (verdict 97); every
capture follows its brackets with no `with`, and no `changes` or `reads` clause, change list or
`captured` is left in any code line, message or comment of the three folders (a search of the built
files for `changes <word>` in code, `with copy of|owns|thread_shared|shares|weakref`, E2105, E2107, E2113,
E2122, E2085 finds only plain English and the history sentences of the comments above).

### Open points

None is for the owner. Step 3's eleven, each settled:

1. The trampoline tag's layout: R15 (`rules.md:901-903`), the lead's (U8).
2. The stop of a used-up `callback(once)` called again: section 2 (`rules.md:1009`), the lead's (F-14,
   U9); the test checks the whole text.
3. E5013's message: the lead's, naming only the promotion (`rules.md:1111`).
4. A `thread_shared` object whose class has a field of a plain function type: E7015 at the line that
   makes the object (`rules.md:698-702`, the lead's, hole 6a's form); its test is T3's in `R12/`.
5. A scoped task's loop name without a word: E3026, whose message gives both words (verdict 93,
   `rules.md:869-871`).
6. Verdict 65's copy of a string loop variable: verdict 93 adds `ref t` for scoped tasks, with no copy.
7. A plain `thread_shared Log` argument beside a function value that holds it: allowed (verdict 89,
   R4 exception 2, `rules.md:307-313`).
8. `owns` on the making line of an `Option[owns T]` field: verdict 85.
9. A change a callee names by a type: void with verdict 86 (no first line names changes).
10. E2040 for two operations: one message, on the first line that needs the bound (`rules.md:830-832`).
11. E7010's second way out: dropped from the message (`rules.md:1106`; T-W6 11).

Settled here (technical, no change to what a program writes or does beyond what a verdict says):
- A typed local or a map value of an `owns dyn I` type is "a place that owns the value", so its making
  line says `owns` (`a: owns dyn Shape = owns Circle(r=3)`, `{1u128: owns Pool[String](...)}`): verdict
  85 ("wherever the place owns the value"); R3's examples name fields, `Option` and lists.
- `ref t` given to `thread.spawn` gets E3026 (the lead's code, worklist section 3), although the
  function then also holds a lent view (E7015): the two fit one line, and E3026's fix, `copy of t`,
  compiles (verdict 73).
- E3040 for a widget handed to C and used again, in the form of section 3's E3040 message; its way out
  gives C another widget, since a foreign `Widget` has no `Clone`.
